    REQUIRED for the workstations to charge at full 240W speed?**

<!-- -->

1.  Enable USB PD 3.1 in the workstation's BIOS settings

2.  Replace cables with EPR-rated USB-C cables supporting USB PD 3.1

3.  Use two 100W cables simultaneously for combined 200W

4.  Install a firmware update on the existing USB-C cables

### Answer Key

Answer & Explanation

1.  **B** or **C)** Dual-channel requires matched DIMMs installed
    across **both channels** — one in Channel A and one in Channel B
    (e.g., **A1+B1** or **A2+B2**). Installing both in the same channel
    (A1+A2) gives only single-channel bandwidth

2.  **C)** 24-pin ATX - The 24-pin ATX connector is the main motherboard
    power connector. The 8-pin EPS powers the CPU separately.

3.  **B)** BIOS/UEFI needs updating - Older BIOS versions may not
    include NVMe support drivers. Update BIOS first. NVMe drives do not
    use SATA cables or PCIe power connectors.

4.  **C)** RAID 6 - Double parity, minimum 4 drives, survives two
    simultaneous failures. RAID 10 also uses 4 drives but can only
    survive failures in different mirror pairs.

5.  **C)** Fuser assembly - The fuser melts toner into paper using heat
    and pressure. If it fails, toner doesn’t bond and smears easily.

6.  **C)** Inverter - The inverter converts DC to AC for the CCFL
    backlight. If the external monitor works, the GPU is fine. A failed
    inverter dims or kills the internal display only.

7.  **C)** Magnetic tape - Degaussing only works on magnetic media.
    SSDs, USB drives, and optical discs are unaffected.

8.  **B)** DHCP server is unreachable - APIPA (169.254.x.x) is
    automatically assigned when no DHCP server responds. The Wi-Fi
    connection may be active but DHCP failed.

9.  **A)** Windows 10 Pro - Domain join requires Pro or Enterprise. Home
    cannot join an AD domain.

10. **B)** DISM /Online /Cleanup-Image /RestoreHealth - DISM repairs the
    Windows component store that sfc relies on. Always run DISM before
    retrying sfc.

11. **C)** Reliability Monitor - Shows app crashes and failed updates on
    a timeline. Event Viewer is more detailed but not visual. Task
    Manager shows current processes.

12. **B)** Browser hijacker - This is a classic browser hijacker
    symptom. Fix: malware removal + browser settings reset.

13. **C)** Differential backup - Only needs the last full + the latest
    differential. Incremental requires the last full + every incremental
    in sequence.

14. **D)** Secure Erase - Multi-pass overwrite is unreliable on SSDs
    (wear-leveling). Secure Erase uses the drive’s firmware to reset all
    cells. Degaussing has no effect on SSDs.

15. **B)** Whaling - Whaling specifically targets executives (like a CEO
    requesting action). Spear-phishing targets specific individuals
    generally; whaling targets senior leadership specifically.

16. **D)** WPA3 - WPA3 uses Simultaneous Authentication of Equals (SAE)
    to replace WPA2’s PSK, providing stronger protection against offline
    dictionary attacks.

17. **C)** Disable System Restore - Step 3 of the 7-step process. System
    Restore must be disabled first so malware cannot hide in restore
    points and reinfect after removal.

18. **C)** Certificate of Destruction - Required for regulated data
    (PHI, PII) to prove compliance with HIPAA and other regulations.

19. **B)** BitLocker - Full-disk encryption renders data unreadable
    without the recovery key, even if the drive is removed and read in
    another machine.

20. **B)** Data retained, violates PCI-DSS - PCI-DSS prohibits sharing
    cardholder data with unauthorized third parties. Public AI tools are
    not approved processors of payment card data.

21. **B)** 22 - SSH uses port 22. Telnet (23) is unencrypted. RDP
    is 3389. HTTPS is 443.

22. **C)** IPMI / iDRAC - Out-of-band management tools allow
    hardware-level server access regardless of OS state.
    RDP/VNC/TeamViewer require a running OS.

23. **C)** Disable WPS entirely - WPS has an exploitable 8-digit PIN
    vulnerability and should always be disabled regardless of which
    Wi-Fi security protocol is in use.

24. **C)** IMAP - IMAP keeps email on the server and syncs across all
    devices. POP3 downloads to one device and removes from server.

25. **B)** Back up the data immediately. A rhythmic clicking sound
    indicates an imminent physical hard drive (HDD) failure (head
    crash). Data recovery takes priority over diagnostics

26. **C)** SaaS (Software as a Service). The provider manages
    everything, and the user simply consumes the application (like
    Microsoft 365 or Gmail).

27. **B)** Failing fuser assembly or drum. Ghosted images are typically
    caused by a failing fuser or a bad cleaning blade on the imaging
    drum. *(Though both the fuser and drum can cause ghosting, on the
    exam, the drum is more likely to be the correct answer.)*

28. **B)** ipconfig /release followed by ipconfig /renew. This drops the
    current IP and forces the DORA process to request a new one.

29. **C)** A mantrap. A mantrap uses two interlocking doors to ensure
    only one authorized person can enter a secure area at a time,
    specifically preventing tailgating.

30. **D)** Plenum-rated cable. Plenum cables have a special
    fire-retardant jacket that produces less toxic smoke when burned,
    making them legally required for HVAC air spaces.

31. **C)** Power off and isolate. A bulging Li-ion battery is a severe
    fire hazard. Never squeeze, puncture, or charge a swollen battery.

32. **C)** Bridged. Bridged networking connects the VM directly to the
    physical network, allowing it to request its own IP address from the
    local DHCP server.

33. **A)** Ransomware. Malware that locks the system or encrypts files
    and demands payment is ransomware.

34. **C)** Verify full system functionality. Step 6 of the
    troubleshooting process is to verify the fix works and check that no
    other systems were broken in the process, *followed* by documenting
    the findings.

35. **B)** The device has a Link-Local address for local segment
    communication only. fe80::/10 is the Link-Local prefix in IPv6,
    automatically assigned to all IPv6 interfaces. Link-Local addresses
    are NOT routable beyond the local network segment, which is why the
    device cannot reach the internet.

36. **C)** USB PD 2.0 maxes out at 100W (20V @ 5A), insufficient for
    180W requirement. The dock negotiates the maximum power it can
    provide (100W), which is less than the laptop needs for full-speed
    charging. The solution is to use a USB PD 3.1 charger rated for at
    least 180W, or use the laptop's dedicated AC power adapter.

37. **A)** Wi-Fi 6E operates in the uncongested 6 GHz band with no
    legacy device interference. Wi-Fi 6E extends Wi-Fi 6 (802.11ax) into
    the 6 GHz frequency band, which is exclusive to Wi-Fi 6E devices.
    The 2.4 GHz and 5 GHz bands are extremely congested with legacy
    devices, Bluetooth, microwaves, and competing networks.

38. **D)** A Thread Border Router to bridge Thread and IP networks. A
    Thread Border Router is required to connect the Thread mesh network
    to other IP networks (Wi-Fi, Ethernet, internet)

39. **B)** SLAAC (Stateless Address Autoconfiguration). SLAAC (Stateless
    Address Autoconfiguration) allows IPv6 devices to automatically
    configure their own addresses without a DHCP server. SLAAC is
    stateless (no server tracks assignments), while DHCPv6 is stateful
    (server manages and tracks).

40. **B)** Replace cables with EPR-rated USB-C cables supporting USB PD
    3.1. USB PD 3.1 with Extended Power Range (EPR) supports up to 240W
    at higher voltages. The cable contains an e-marker chip that stores
    its power capability information, which the device and charger query
    during power negotiation.

# Skills Test - Performance-Based Scenarios

These scenarios simulate the Performance-Based Questions (PBQs) found on
the CompTIA A+ exam. PBQs require you to apply knowledge to realistic
situations - not just recall facts. Each scenario describes a real-world
task. Read carefully, complete the task, then check the Answer Key at
the end.

**Format:** Step-ordering, fill-in, configuration, matching, and
decision-making

**Exam Weight:** PBQs = 3-5 questions on the actual exam; they count the
same as multiple-choice

**Coverage:** 10 scenarios covering both Core 1 and Core 2 domains

**Strategy:** On the real exam, flag PBQs and return to them after
finishing multiple-choice questions

### Scenario 1: Malware Incident Response

**Domain:** Core 2 - Security

SITUATION:

A user calls the help desk. Their desktop is showing pop-up warnings
claiming the FBI has locked their computer and demanding a \$500
cryptocurrency payment to unlock it. The machine is connected to the
corporate LAN. You have confirmed it is malware.

**YOUR TASK:** The steps below are out of order. Write the correct
sequence number (1-7) next to each step.

> ● Educate the user on how to avoid similar infections in the future.
>
> ● Run your anti-malware scanning and removal tools.
>
> ● Re-enable System Restore and create a new clean restore point.
>
> ● Identify and research the malware symptoms.
>
> ● Disable System Restore on Windows to prevent the malware from
> sheltering in restore points.
>
> ● Schedule additional follow-up scans and apply any outstanding
> updates.
>
> ● Quarantine the infected system - disconnect it from the corporate
> LAN immediately.

**Scenario 1 ANSWER KEY:**

**A:** 7461352

> ● Identify and research the malware symptoms.
>
> ● Quarantine the infected system (disconnect from the LAN
> immediately).
>
> ● Disable System Restore on Windows.
>
> ● Run anti-malware scanning and removal tools.
>
> ● Schedule additional follow-up scans and apply updates.
>
> ● Re-enable System Restore and create a new clean restore point.
>
> ● Educate the user.
>
> **⚠️ Exam Trap:** Steps 3 and 4 are the most commonly swapped - you
> MUST disable System Restore BEFORE scanning, or the malware can hide
> in restore points and reinfect after removal.

### Scenario 2: SOHO Router Security Audit

**Domain:** Core 2 - Network Security

SITUATION:

You are setting up a new wireless router for a small dental office. The
router is brand new, straight out of the box. The office handles patient
records (PHI) on workstations connected to this network. Your job is to
secure it before going live.

**YOUR TASK:** Review each current setting below. For each one, decide
whether to CHANGE, DISABLE, or LEAVE AS-IS, and explain your reasoning.

1.  Admin Username — Current value: admin
2.  Admin Password — Current value: password
3.  Default SSID — Current value: NETGEAR-5G-23A1
4.  Wireless Security — Current value: WPA2-TKIP
5.  WPS (Wi-Fi Protected Setup) — Current value: Enabled
6.  Remote Management (WAN access) — Current value: Enabled
7.  Guest Network — Current value: Disabled
8.  Firmware Version — Current value: 1.0.0 (latest is 3.2.1)
9.  UPnP (Universal Plug and Play) — Current value: Enabled
10. SPI Firewall — Current value: Enabled

**Scenario 2 ANSWER KEY:**

> ● Admin Username → **CHANGE.** Default ‘admin’ is publicly known.
> Create a unique admin username.
>
> ● Admin Password → **CHANGE.** ‘password’ is the first thing an
> attacker tries.
>
> ● Default SSID → **CHANGE.** ‘NETGEAR-5G-23A1’ reveals the router
> brand — attackers target known vulnerabilities.
>
> ● Wireless Security → **CHANGE.** WPA2-TKIP is outdated. Switch to
> WPA2-AES or WPA3.
>
> ● WPS → **DISABLE.** WPS has a known 8-digit PIN brute-force
> vulnerability. Always disable.
>
> ● Remote Management → **DISABLE.** WAN access to the admin panel is a
> direct attack vector.
>
> ● Guest Network → **CHANGE (Enable).** IoT devices and visitors must
> be isolated from patient record workstations.
>
> ● Firmware → **CHANGE (Update).** Unpatched firmware contains known
> exploits. Update to 3.2.1 immediately.
>
> ● UPnP → **DISABLE.** UPnP can be exploited to automatically open
> ports. Disable in security-conscious environments.
>
> ● SPI Firewall → **LEAVE AS-IS.** Already enabled — stateful packet
> inspection should always be on.

### Scenario 3: Network Connectivity Troubleshooting

**Domain:** Core 1 - Networking

SITUATION:

A user calls saying they have “no internet.” They are on a Windows 11
workstation connected via Ethernet. Other users on the same floor are
working fine. You remote into the machine. You open Command Prompt and
type ipconfig — the result shows: IP Address: 169.254.12.55 / Subnet
Mask: 255.255.0.0 / Default Gateway: (blank)

**YOUR TASK:** Answer each diagnostic question in sequence. Select the
correct action and explain the reasoning.

> ● **Q1:** What does the 169.254.x.x address immediately tell you?

1)  The DNS server is down

<!-- -->

2)  The DHCP server did not respond — this is an APIPA address

<!-- -->

3)  The user has a static IP that conflicts with another device

<!-- -->

4)  The network adapter is disabled

> ● **Q2:** What is your next command to attempt resolving this?

1)  ping 8.8.8.8

<!-- -->

2)  tracert google.com

<!-- -->

3)  ipconfig /release followed by ipconfig /renew

<!-- -->

4)  netstat -a

> ● **Q3:** After running the command, the IP is still 169.254.x.x. What
> do you check next?

1)  Replace the Ethernet cable immediately

<!-- -->

2)  Verify the DHCP server/router is online and check if the Ethernet
    cable is fully seated

<!-- -->

3)  Reinstall Windows network drivers

<!-- -->

4)  Call the ISP

> ● **Q4:** You swap the cable and run ipconfig /renew again. The user
> now gets 192.168.10.45. What should you verify before closing the
> ticket?

1)  Check that the user can ping the default gateway AND open a website

<!-- -->

2)  Simply confirm the IP changed — that is sufficient

<!-- -->

3)  Run sfc /scannow to check for system file corruption

<!-- -->

4)  Reboot the router

**Scenario 3 ANSWER KEY:**

> Q1 → **B.**
>
> APIPA (169.254.x.x) = the device broadcast for a DHCP server and got
> no response. This is not a DNS or static IP issue.
>
> Q2 → **C.**
>
> ipconfig /release drops the current lease; ipconfig /renew forces a
> new DHCP request (DORA process).
>
> Q3 → **B.**
>
> If renewing failed, the DHCP server may be unreachable OR the physical
> connection is the problem. Check both — a bad cable means the DORA
> discover never reaches the router.
>
> Q4 → **A.**
>
> Verify full functionality: the user should be able to ping the gateway
> (local connectivity) AND load a website (DNS + internet working).
> Never close a ticket on an IP change alone.

### Scenario 4: RAM Upgrade & Dual-Channel Configuration

**Domain:** Core 1 - Hardware

SITUATION:

You are upgrading a desktop workstation. The motherboard has 4 DIMM
slots labeled A1, A2, B1, B2. The manual states: “For dual-channel
operation, install matched DIMMs in slots A1+B1 (Channel 1) or A2+B2
(Channel 2). For 4-DIMM dual-channel, populate all four slots with
matched pairs.” Currently installed: one 8GB DDR5 stick in slot A1.

**YOUR TASK:** For each upgrade scenario below, specify which slots to
populate and whether dual-channel will be active.

1.  Add one more 8GB DDR5 stick (same model) — Slots? Dual-channel?
2.  Add two more 8GB DDR5 sticks (same model) — Slots? Dual-channel?
3.  Replace all RAM: install 4× 16GB DDR5 — Slots? Dual-channel?
4.  Add one 16GB DDR5 stick (different model/speed) — Slots?
    Dual-channel?
5.  Add one 8GB DDR4 stick (different generation) — Slots? Dual-channel?

**Scenario 4 ANSWER KEY:**

1.  Add one 8GB DDR5 (same model) → Populate A1 + B1. **YES**,
    dual-channel active. Matched pair in the correct channel pair.
2.  Add two more 8GB DDR5 (same model) → Populate A1 + A2 + B1 + B2.
    **YES**, dual-channel active. Four matched sticks across both
    channels.
3.  Replace with 4× 16GB DDR5 → Populate A1 + A2 + B1 + B2. **YES**,
    dual-channel active. Matched quad-channel set.
4.  Add 16GB DDR5 (different model) → If installed, runs in
    single-channel only — mismatched size prevents dual-channel. **NOT
    recommended.**
5.  Add 8GB DDR4 → **CANNOT DO THIS.** DDR4 and DDR5 have different
    notch positions and are physically incompatible. Will not fit in a
    DDR5 slot.

> **⚠️ Exam Trap:** Dual-channel requires matched DIMMs in the CORRECT
> slot pair — A1+B1 or A2+B2. Putting two sticks in A1+A2 (same channel)
> gives you single-channel only.

### Scenario 5: Mobile Email Account Configuration

**Domain:** Core 1 - Mobile Sync

SITUATION:

You are configuring email on an employee’s new iPhone. They need access
to both their personal Gmail (IMAP) and the company’s Microsoft Exchange
server. They access email from their phone, laptop, and desktop — all
devices must stay synchronized.

**YOUR TASK:** Complete the configuration for each account with the
correct protocol, port numbers, and encryption setting.

1.  Protocol to use — Gmail? Exchange?
2.  Incoming port (encrypted) — Gmail? Exchange?
3.  Outgoing port (client submission) — Gmail? Exchange?
4.  Encryption required? — Gmail? Exchange?
5.  Syncs contacts & calendar too? — Gmail? Exchange?
6.  Best for multi-device access? — Gmail? Exchange?

**Scenario 5 ANSWER KEY:**

> ● Protocol → Gmail: **IMAP** Exchange: **Exchange ActiveSync (EAS)**
>
> ● Incoming port (encrypted) → Gmail: **993 (IMAPS)** Exchange: EAS
> uses HTTPS (**port 443**) — no separate incoming port
>
> ● Outgoing port → Gmail: **587 (SMTP with STARTTLS)** or 465 (implicit
> SSL) Exchange: Handled by EAS — same single connection
>
> ● Encryption → Both: **YES** — always use SSL/TLS. Never configure
> email without encryption.
>
> ● Syncs contacts & calendar → Gmail (IMAP): **Email only** (need
> separate CardDAV/CalDAV for contacts/calendar) Exchange EAS: **YES** —
> email + contacts + calendar in one connection.
>
> ● Best for multi-device → Both: YES for email. Exchange EAS is the
> clear winner for corporate because one connection syncs everything.
>
> **⚠️ Exam Trap:** EAS (Exchange ActiveSync) is the corporate standard
> because it handles email + contacts + calendar over a single protocol.
> IMAP alone only handles email.

### Scenario 6: Data Destruction Method Selection

**Domain:** Core 2 - Data Disposal

SITUATION:

Your company is decommissioning a variety of devices. Compliance
requires proper data destruction before disposal. Some devices will be
reused; others will not. Match each device and situation to the single
BEST destruction method.

**YOUR TASK:** For each scenario, write the most appropriate method.
Choose from: Secure Erase, Zero Fill, Multi-Pass Overwrite (DoD),
Degaussing, Physical Shredding, Cryptographic Erasure, Factory Reset.

1.  A 5-year-old HDD from an accounting workstation. The drive will be
    donated to a school after wiping.
2.  An SSD from the HR director’s laptop. Contains employee PII. The
    laptop is being repurposed internally.
3.  A box of 50 old magnetic backup tapes from the server room. They
    will not be reused.
4.  A corporate iPhone that was issued to an employee (hardware
    encryption enabled). Being reassigned to a new hire.
5.  A SATA SSD from a healthcare workstation containing PHI. Being
    disposed of permanently. Compliance audit required.
6.  A USB flash drive found in the lost and found. Unknown contents,
    will not be reused.
7.  An NVMe SSD from a workstation that will be repurposed for another
    department.

**Scenario 6 ANSWER KEY:**

> ● HDD for donation → **Zero Fill (Single-Pass Overwrite).** Drive is
> reused — physical destruction not needed. Meets NIST SP 800-88
> standard.
>
> ● SSD from HR laptop (repurposed) → **Secure Erase (ATA firmware
> command).** Multi-pass overwrite is unreliable on SSDs due to
> wear-leveling. Secure Erase resets all cells.
>
> ● Magnetic backup tapes (disposed) → **Degaussing + Shredding.**
> Degaussing destroys the magnetic data; shredding provides physical
> assurance. Note: degaussing alone is acceptable but physical
> destruction adds security.
>
> ● iPhone with hardware encryption (reassigned) → **Factory Reset.**
> Hardware encryption + factory reset destroys the encryption key,
> rendering data unrecoverable. Fast and effective.
>
> ● SSD from healthcare workstation (PHI, compliance audit) → **Physical
> Shredding + Certificate of Destruction.** PHI requires documented
> proof of disposal for HIPAA compliance. Shredding is the most thorough
> method.
>
> ● Unknown USB flash drive (disposed) → **Physical Shredding.** Unknown
> contents, not reused — shredding is fastest and most thorough.
>
> ● NVMe SSD (repurposed) → **Secure Erase.** Same reasoning as \#2 —
> Secure Erase is the correct method for any flash-based storage being
> reused.
>
> **⚠️ Exam Trap:** Never use Multi-Pass Overwrite (DoD) on SSDs —
> wear-leveling means the firmware may skip sectors. Always use Secure
> Erase for SSDs.

### Scenario 7: Virtual Machine Network Mode Configuration

**Domain:** Core 1 - Virtualization

SITUATION:

You are a lab instructor setting up virtual machines for a security
training course. Different VMs need different levels of network access
based on their purpose. You are using VMware Workstation (Type 2
hypervisor) on a Windows 11 host.

**YOUR TASK:** For each VM purpose below, select the correct network
mode. Choose from: NAT, Bridged, Host-Only, Internal Network.

1.  A student needs to practice web browsing and downloading security
    tools from the internet. The VM should share the host’s internet but
    not be directly visible on the network.
2.  A simulated web server VM must get its own IP address from the
    office DHCP server and be accessible to other real computers on the
    network.
3.  A malware analysis sandbox. The VM must have ZERO access to the
    internet or the host machine — completely isolated.
4.  Two Kali Linux VMs need to attack and defend each other in a
    simulated penetration test. They should only communicate with each
    other, not the host or internet.
5.  A monitoring VM needs to communicate with the host machine for log
    collection but must not touch the external network.

**Scenario 7 ANSWER KEY:**

> ● Internet access, shares host IP → **NAT.** VM uses host’s IP via
> translation. Safest default for internet-connected student VMs.
>
> ● Own IP, visible on real network → **Bridged.** VM gets its own IP
> from the physical network’s DHCP server, fully visible to other
> devices.
>
> ● Malware sandbox — completely isolated → **Internal Network.** VMs
> communicate only with each other. No host access, no internet. Most
> secure isolation.
>
> ● Two VMs attack/defend each other only → **Internal Network.** Same
> reasoning — VMs communicate only with each other, not the host or
> internet.
>
> ● VM to host only, no internet → **Host-Only.** VM communicates only
> with the host machine. External network and internet are blocked.
>
> **⚠️ Exam Trap:** NAT = internet access, shared IP. Bridged = own IP,
> on the real network. Host-Only = host only. Internal = VMs only.
> Remember this hierarchy.

### Scenario 8: Windows Edition Requirements

**Domain:** Core 2 - Operating Systems

SITUATION:

You work IT support for a mid-size company. The purchasing manager asks
for help ordering the correct Windows edition for five different
employee types. Choosing the wrong edition will mean features don’t work
— or they overspend.

**YOUR TASK:** For each employee requirement, specify the MINIMUM
Windows edition needed (Home, Pro, or Enterprise) and identify the key
feature driving that choice.

1.  A receptionist who only needs email, web browsing, and
    Microsoft 365. No IT management needed. Budget is a priority.
2.  A developer who needs to run Linux virtual machines locally using
    Hyper-V. Must also join the corporate Active Directory domain.
3.  A C-suite executive whose laptop must encrypt the entire drive with
    BitLocker in case it is lost or stolen.
4.  A large hospital network that needs to centrally manage 2,000 PCs
    using Group Policy, deploy apps silently via SCCM, and use
    DirectAccess for VPN-less remote connectivity.
5.  A warehouse employee who uses only one application on a kiosk-style
    machine (Assigned Access / kiosk mode). The machine must never show
    the desktop.

**Scenario 8 ANSWER KEY:**

> ● Receptionist → **HOME.** Basic consumer features only. No domain
> join, no BitLocker, no Group Policy needed. Most cost-effective
> choice.
>
> ● Developer (Hyper-V + AD domain) → **PRO.** Both Hyper-V and Active
> Directory domain join require Windows Pro minimum.
>
> ● Executive (BitLocker) → **PRO.** BitLocker full-disk encryption
> requires Windows Pro, Pro for Workstations, or Enterprise — NOT Home.
>
> ● Hospital (SCCM + DirectAccess + Group Policy, 2000 PCs) →
> **ENTERPRISE.** DirectAccess and volume management at scale
> (SCCM/WSUS) are Enterprise features. Also requires volume licensing.
>
> ● Kiosk (Assigned Access) → **PRO.** Assigned Access (kiosk mode) to
> lock a machine to a single app requires Windows Pro or Enterprise.
>
> **⚠️ Exam Trap:** BitLocker + Domain Join + Group Policy + Hyper-V +
> Remote Desktop Host + Assigned Access = Pro minimum. DirectAccess +
> AppLocker + LTSC = Enterprise only.

### Scenario 9: Backup Strategy Design

**Domain:** Core 2 - Backup & Recovery

SITUATION:

You are the IT administrator for a 30-person accounting firm. The firm
generates critical financial records Monday through Friday. A ransomware
attack last year destroyed a week of work. Management has mandated a
formal backup strategy that: (a) meets the 3-2-1 rule, (b) allows
restoring any single day’s work within 2 hours, and (c) minimizes daily
backup time.

**YOUR TASK:** Complete the backup schedule and answer the follow-up
questions.

1.  Sunday night — Backup type? Storage location? Retention period?
2.  Monday–Friday nights — Backup type? Storage location? Retention
    period?
3.  End of month — Backup type? Storage location? Retention period?

**Follow-up Questions:**

> ● What specific backup type meets the 3-2-1 rule? Explain the 3
> elements.
>
> ● To restore Wednesday’s work, which backups do you need?
>
> ● Why is incremental chosen over differential for daily backups in
> this scenario?
>
> ● If the office burns down, how is the data protected?

**Scenario 9 ANSWER KEY — Backup Schedule:**

> ● Sunday night → **FULL backup** External NAS + offsite cloud Keep 4
> weekly fulls (1 month)
>
> ● Monday–Friday nights → **INCREMENTAL backups** Local external drive
> Keep 7 days of incrementals
>
> ● End of month → **FULL backup** Offsite cloud only Keep 12 monthly
> fulls (1 year)

**Follow-up Answers:**

> ● **3-2-1 Rule** → 3 copies of data: the live data, the local backup,
> and the cloud backup. On 2 different media types: external drive +
> cloud. With 1 copy off-site: cloud storage.
>
> ● **Restore Wednesday** → Need Sunday’s full backup + Monday
> incremental + Tuesday incremental + Wednesday incremental (4 total
> restore operations).
>
> ● **Why incremental over differential** → Incremental backups are
> faster to create daily (only changes since the LAST backup). This
> minimizes backup window during business hours. Differential would grow
> in size each day across the week.
>
> ● **Office fire protection** → The offsite cloud copy. This is why the
> 3-2-1 rule mandates 1 copy off-site — a local-only strategy fails
> completely in a disaster.

### Scenario 10: Laser Printer Component Diagnosis

**Domain:** Core 1 - Printers

SITUATION:

You support a fleet of laser printers in a corporate office. Users have
been calling in with various print quality issues. Each symptom points
to a specific failed component in the laser printing process. Your job
is to identify the correct component and recommend a fix.

**YOUR TASK:** For each symptom, identify the most likely failed
component and the recommended fix. Use the laser printing process
(Processing → Charging → Exposing → Developing → Transferring → Fusing →
Cleaning) to guide your reasoning.

> 1\. Pages come out blank — no text or image at all.
>
> 2\. Toner smears and rubs off the page when touched.
>
> 3\. A faint “ghost” of the previous page appears behind the current
> print.
>
> 4\. Vertical black or white streaks run down every page.
>
> 5\. The printer feeds paper but immediately jams at the entry point
> every time.
>
> 6\. Print quality looks perfect but the machine won’t pull paper from
> the tray.

**Scenario 10 ANSWER KEY:**

> ● Blank pages → **Failed laser assembly (or empty toner).** The laser
> writes the image onto the drum — if it fails, no image forms. Also
> check toner level first (easy win).
>
> ● Toner smears when touched → **Fuser assembly failure.** The fuser’s
> heat/pressure rollers melt toner into the paper. If the fuser fails,
> toner sits loose on the surface and smears.
>
> ● Ghost image from previous page → **Fuser failure (incomplete fusing)
> OR dirty/worn imaging drum.** A residual charge on the drum attracts
> toner in the ghost pattern. *(Though both the fuser and drum can cause
> ghosting, on the exam, the drum is more likely to be the correct
> answer.)*
>
> ● Vertical streaks → **Scratched/dirty imaging drum OR dirty primary
> corona/charging roller.** A scratch on the drum creates a consistent
> line on every page.
>
> ● Paper jams immediately on entry → **Worn or dirty separation pad or
> feed rollers.** These components separate individual sheets — when
> worn, sheets either double-feed or jam.
>
> ● Won’t pull paper from tray → **Worn or dirty pickup rollers.**
> Pickup rollers grab paper from the tray. When worn smooth, they spin
> without gripping the paper.
>
> **⚠️ Exam Trap:** Smearing = fuser. Ghost images = fuser or drum.
> Streaks = drum or corona. Paper feed problems = pickup rollers. Blank
> pages = laser assembly or toner.

## Skills Test - Scoring Guide

Use the rubric below to assess your performance. Each scenario is worth
10 points for a total of 100 points.

Score Ranges:

> ● **90–100 — Exam Ready:** You have strong conceptual + applied
> knowledge. Focus your final review on the Quick Reference Cards and
> port numbers.
>
> ● **75–89 — Almost There:** Review the scenarios you missed. Re-read
> the relevant module and attempt those scenarios again without looking
> at the answers.
>
> ● **60–74 — Needs Reinforcement:** Return to the core content modules
> for the scenarios you struggled with. Focus on the ‘Pro Tips’ and
> ‘Exam Traps’ sections.
>
> ● **Below 60 — Back to Basics:** Complete a full re-read of all
> modules before re-attempting the Skills Test. Consider adding hands-on
> lab practice.

**Which Scenarios Map to Which Exam Domains:**

1.  Scenario 1 — Malware Response → Core 2: Security (28%)
2.  Scenario 2 — SOHO Router Audit → Core 2: Security - Network Security
3.  Scenario 3 — Network Troubleshooting Flowchart → Core 1: Hardware &
    Network Troubleshooting (28%)
4.  Scenario 4 — RAM Installation Lab → Core 1: Hardware (25%)
5.  Scenario 5 — Email Configuration → Core 1: Mobile Devices / Core 2:
    Operational Procedures
6.  Scenario 6 — Data Destruction Matrix → Core 2: Operational
    Procedures (21%)
7.  Scenario 7 — VM Network Modes → Core 1: Virtualization & Cloud (11%)
8.  Scenario 8 — Windows Edition Requirements → Core 2: Operating
    Systems (28%)
9.  Scenario 9 — Backup Strategy Design → Core 2: Operational Procedures
10. Scenario 10 — Laser Printer Diagnosis → Core 1: Hardware & Network
    Troubleshooting

**Final PBQ Tips for Exam Day:**

> ● Skip PBQs first — flag them and do all multiple-choice questions
> first to build confidence and bank time.
>
> ● Read every word of the scenario — the answer is almost always
> embedded in the details.
>
> ● Use the process of elimination — even on simulations, removing wrong
> options narrows your decision.
>
> ● If you cannot complete a PBQ fully, partial credit is awarded —
> enter your best attempt before time expires.
>
> ● There is no penalty for wrong answers — always submit something.

# The Next Steps

You Are Ready! If you have made it through this guide, completed the
practice tests, and practiced your troubleshooting steps, **you have
what it takes to pass the CompTIA A+ exams.** The A+ is a marathon, not
a sprint. It tests a massive ocean of information, but it is a shallow
ocean. You do not need to be an engineer who can build a motherboard
from scratch; you simply need to be a sharp, systematic problem solver
who understands how the pieces fit together.

### Your Pre-Exam Checklist:

> ● **Schedule the Exam:** Do not wait until you feel “100% perfectly
> ready” - that day never comes. Put a date on the calendar. A deadline
> forces you to focus.
>
> ● **Review the Exam Objectives:** Go to the official CompTIA website
> and download the Core 1 (220-1201) and Core 2 (220-1202) exam
> objectives. Read through the bullet points. If you can explain a
> bullet point out loud for 30 seconds, you know it.
>
> ● **Memorize the “Cheat Sheet” Data:** The night before your exam,
> review the mnemonics section in this book. Memorize your ports (Port
> 22, 53, 80, 443, 3389), your Wi-Fi speeds, and the 6-step
> troubleshooting process.
>
> ● **Plan for PBQs:** Expect the first 3 to 5 questions on your test to
> be Performance-Based Questions (drag-and-drop or simulation).
>
> **✔ Pro Tip:** Skip them immediately. Flag them and do the
> multiple-choice questions first to build your confidence, then return
> to the PBQs at the end.
>
> ● **Rest:** Do not cram the morning of the test. A rested brain
> recalls information much faster than an exhausted one.

### Ways to Save Money on Your CompTIA A+ Exam

Earning your certification is an investment in your IT career, but exam
vouchers and study materials can add up quickly. Fortunately, there are
several smart ways to reduce costs without compromising on quality
training.

### 1. CompTIA Bundles

CompTIA frequently offers discounted bundles that include:

> ● Exam vouchers
>
> ● Retake vouchers
>
> ● Practice tests
>
> ● CertMaster training tools

These bundles are often cheaper than buying items separately and provide
peace of mind if you need a second attempt.

### 2. Student Discounts

If you’re enrolled in a college, university, or technical school, check
CompTIA’s academic purchasing options. Students can save **35–55%** off
retail prices by verifying their status through CompTIA’s academic
verification program.

### 3. Employer Reimbursement

Many employers and unions offer:

> ● Tuition reimbursement
>
> ● Professional development funding
>
> ● Certification reimbursement programs

If you work in customer service, technical support, or IT, ask your HR
department whether certification assistance is available.

### 4. Workforce Development Programs

State unemployment offices, nonprofits, and workforce agencies often
provide **low-cost or fully funded IT training** that includes CompTIA
vouchers. These programs are ideal for:

> ● Career changers
>
> ● Veterans
>
> ● Unemployed workers
>
> ● Individuals entering technology for the first time

### 5. Watch for Promotions

CompTIA occasionally runs limited-time sales on:

> ● Exam vouchers
>
> ● Study bundles
>
> ● Training products
>
> **✔ Pro Tip:** Combine a student discount with a bundle offer when
> possible. It can cut your total cost by more than half!

### Final Advice

Do not rush to schedule your exam before you are ready. A failed exam
costs more than taking extra time to study properly. Use practice exams,
review weak areas, and schedule your test when you consistently score
well on practice assessments.

Certification is an investment in yourself - and the skills you gain can
open doors to help desk, technical support, networking, cybersecurity,
and systems administration careers.

Welcome to IT

Earning your CompTIA A+ certification is more than just passing a test -
it is the universal starting line for a career in Information
Technology. Whether your goal is to become a Systems Administrator, a
Cloud Engineer, or a Cybersecurity Analyst, the foundational knowledge
you have built here will stay with you for the rest of your career.

Trust your preparation, read every question carefully, and
systematically eliminate the wrong answers.
